    - align-offset (number) default: -5 — Offset from the start or end alignment, in pixels
    - layout ("vertical" | "horizontal") default: "vertical" — Layout direction. Use horizontal for icon toolbars
    - size ("sm" | "md" | "lg") default: "md" — Size of horizontal menu items (icon size + padding)
    - @close-auto-focus ((event: Event) => void) — Emitted before focus returns to the trigger; call event.preventDefault() to keep it from moving there
  MenuItem:
    - description (string) — Second line under the label (or the description slot). The label turns semibold on its own
    - disabled (boolean) default: false — Disable the item
    - danger (boolean) default: false — Destructive action: the item is drawn in the danger color
    - @select ((event: Event) => void) — Called when the item is selected; call event.preventDefault() to keep the menu open
    - @click ((event: MouseEvent) => void) — Called when the item is clicked; also makes the item interactive
  MenuCheckboxItem:
    - checked / v-model:checked (boolean | "indeterminate") default: false — Checked state
    - @checked-change ((checked: boolean) => void) — Emitted with update:checked when the checkbox toggles
    - @select ((event: Event) => void) — Call event.preventDefault() to keep the menu open
    - indicator-position ("start" | "end") default: "end" — Checkbox position
    - indicator-variant ("solid" | "ghost") default: "solid" — Indicator style
    - disabled (boolean) default: false — Disable the item

### Overview
```vue
<Menu>
  <MenuTrigger>
    <Button color="primary" size="lg" variant="ghost">Sample menu <ChevronDownMd /></Button>
  </MenuTrigger>
  <MenuContent :width="210" :min-width="210">
    <MenuItem description="sam.smith@gmail.com">Sam Smith</MenuItem>
    <MenuSeparator />
    <MenuLink href="#">Your profile</MenuLink>
    <MenuLink href="#">Terms & policies</MenuLink>
    <MenuItem disabled @select="() => {}">Feature flags</MenuItem>
    <MenuSeparator />
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}">Logout</MenuItem>
  </MenuContent>
</Menu>
```

### Natural sizing
Menu supports several sizing strategies:Natural sizing: use min-width="auto" to have the menu fit naturally to the size of the content.Fixed size: use width and min-width to set the menu to a fixed size, regardless of content size.Flexible sizing: min-width can be used by itself to describe a menu that must be a given size, but can grow larger depending on its content.Max sizing: apply max-width to the children of MenuContent. maxWidth isn't exposed as a prop because it's reserved for the custom properties used in collision detection.
```vue
<Menu>
  <MenuTrigger>
    <Button color="primary" size="lg" variant="ghost" class="font-semibold gap-1.5"><Tools /> Tools</Button>
  </MenuTrigger>
  <MenuContent min-width="auto">
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><Functions :height="18" :width="18" /> Function</MenuItem>
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><Search :height="18" :width="18" /> File Search</MenuItem>
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><Globe :height="18" :width="18" /> Web Search</MenuItem>
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><Code :height="18" :width="18" /> Code Interpreter</MenuItem>
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><ImageSquare :height="18" :width="18" /> Image Generation</MenuItem>
  </MenuContent>
</Menu>
```

### Horizontal (icons only)
Set layout="horizontal" on MenuContent for an iOS-style icon toolbar. Give each icon-only item a visually hidden label so screen readers can name the action. On a phone with touch.css the row takes the geometry of the quick actions of an iOS menu (the kit has no icon-only row): equal 56px-tall actions with a 13px symbol, without separators, across at least 238px whatever width and minWidth say, at every size, so the phone view leaves out the size control.
```vue
<Menu>
  <MenuTrigger>
    <Button color="primary" size="lg" variant="ghost">Actions <ChevronDownMd /></Button>
  </MenuTrigger>
  <MenuContent layout="horizontal" :size="size" min-width="auto">
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><EditPencil /><span class="sr-only">Edit</span></MenuItem>
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><SettingsCog /><span class="sr-only">Settings</span></MenuItem>
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><Play /><span class="sr-only">Play</span></MenuItem>
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><UnfoldHorizontal /><span class="sr-only">Expand</span></MenuItem>
  </MenuContent>
</Menu>
```

### Horizontal (icons with labels)
Add text labels below icons for a labeled toolbar. The horizontal layout automatically stacks icon and text vertically within each item. On a phone with touch.css these are the quick actions of an iOS menu: a 13px symbol over a 12px label in equal 56px-tall actions, as wide as the widest label, without separators, across at least 238px whatever width and minWidth say, at every size, so the phone view leaves out the size control.
```vue
<Menu>
  <MenuTrigger>
    <Button color="primary" size="lg" variant="ghost">Actions <ChevronDownMd /></Button>
  </MenuTrigger>
  <MenuContent layout="horizontal" :size="size" min-width="auto">
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><EditPencil /> Edit</MenuItem>
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><SettingsCog /> Settings</MenuItem>
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><Play /> Play</MenuItem>
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><UnfoldHorizontal /> Expand</MenuItem>
  </MenuContent>
</Menu>
```

### Grouped items
Use MenuGroup and MenuLabel to organize related items into labeled sections.
```vue
<Menu>
  <MenuTrigger>
    <Button color="primary" size="lg" variant="ghost" class="font-semibold gap-1.5"><FileDocument /> Templates <ChevronDownMd /></Button>
  </MenuTrigger>
  <MenuContent :min-width="220">
    <MenuGroup>
      <MenuLabel>Active</MenuLabel>
      <MenuLink href="#">Welcome email</MenuLink>
      <MenuLink href="#">Order confirmation</MenuLink>
      <MenuLink href="#">Password reset</MenuLink>
    </MenuGroup>
    <MenuSeparator />
    <MenuGroup>
      <MenuLabel>Draft</MenuLabel>
      <MenuLink href="#">New promo campaign</MenuLink>
      <MenuLink href="#">Account deactivation</MenuLink>
    </MenuGroup>
  </MenuContent>
</Menu>
```

### Submenus
Use MenuSub, MenuSubTrigger, and MenuSubContent for nested menus.
```vue
<Menu>
  <MenuTrigger>
    <Button color="primary" size="lg" variant="ghost">Options <ChevronDownMd /></Button>
  </MenuTrigger>
  <MenuContent :min-width="180">
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}">Edit</MenuItem>
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}">Duplicate</MenuItem>
    <MenuSeparator />
    <MenuSub>
      <MenuSubTrigger>More</MenuSubTrigger>
      <MenuSubContent>
        <MenuItem @select="() => {}">Move to project…</MenuItem>
        <MenuItem @select="() => {}">Move to folder…</MenuItem>
        <MenuSeparator />
        <MenuItem @select="() => {}">Advanced options…</MenuItem>
      </MenuSubContent>
    </MenuSub>
    <MenuSeparator />
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}">Share</MenuItem>
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}">Add to favorites</MenuItem>
    <MenuSeparator />
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}">Delete</MenuItem>
  </MenuContent>
</Menu>
```

### Checkbox items
Use MenuCheckboxItem with v-model:checked and {'@select="(evt) => evt.preventDefault()"'} to keep the menu open. On a phone with touch.css the checkmark leads the row, as in iOS menus, whatever indicatorPosition and indicatorVariant say, so the phone view leaves out those controls.
```vue
<Menu>
  <MenuTrigger>
    <Button variant="ghost" color="primary">Checkbox menu <ChevronDownMd /></Button>
  </MenuTrigger>
  <MenuContent :min-width="200">
    <MenuCheckboxItem
      v-model:checked="settings.showGrid"
      :indicator-position="indicatorPosition"
      :indicator-variant="indicatorVariant"
      @select="(evt) => evt.preventDefault()"
    >
      Show grid lines
    </MenuCheckboxItem>
    <MenuCheckboxItem
      v-model:checked="settings.showLabels"
      :indicator-position="indicatorPosition"
      :indicator-variant="indicatorVariant"
      @select="(evt) => evt.preventDefault()"
    >
      Display labels
    </MenuCheckboxItem>
    <MenuCheckboxItem
      v-model:checked="settings.enableShadows"
      :indicator-position="indicatorPosition"
      :indicator-variant="indicatorVariant"
      @select="(evt) => evt.preventDefault()"
    >
      Enable shadows
    </MenuCheckboxItem>
  </MenuContent>
</Menu>
```

### Radio items
Use MenuRadioGroup and MenuRadioItem for single-selection options. Bind the selection with v-model:value. On a phone with touch.css the checkmark leads the row, as in iOS menus, whatever indicatorPosition says, so the phone view leaves out that control.
```vue
<Menu>
  <MenuTrigger>
    <Button color="primary" variant="ghost">Radio menu <ChevronDownMd /></Button>
  </MenuTrigger>
  <MenuContent align="start" min-width="auto" width="auto">
    <MenuRadioGroup v-model:value="value" :indicator-position="indicatorPosition">
      <MenuRadioItem value="any">Any time</MenuRadioItem>
      <MenuRadioItem value="today" disabled>Today</MenuRadioItem>
      <MenuRadioItem value="last7d">Last 7 days</MenuRadioItem>
      <MenuRadioItem value="last30d">Last 30 days</MenuRadioItem>
      <MenuRadioItem value="last3m">Last 3 months</MenuRadioItem>
    </MenuRadioGroup>
  </MenuContent>
</Menu>
```

### Item actions
Use MenuItemActions and MenuItemAction for hover actions (e.g. delete) on an item.Accessibility note: these actions are not keyboard accessible due to how the menu primitive works, so they should not be critical actions with no other parity.
```vue
<Menu>
  <MenuTrigger>
    <Button color="primary" size="lg" variant="ghost" class="font-semibold gap-1.5"><History /> Conversations</Button>
  </MenuTrigger>
  <MenuContent :width="270">
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}">
      <span class="flex-1 truncate">Sample thread title</span>
      <MenuItemActions>
        <MenuItemAction @click="() => {}"><Trash /></MenuItemAction>
      </MenuItemActions>
    </MenuItem>
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}">
      <span class="flex-1 truncate">Another sample thread title</span>
      <MenuItemActions>
        <MenuItemAction @click="() => {}"><Trash /></MenuItemAction>
      </MenuItemActions>
    </MenuItem>
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}">
      <span class="flex-1 truncate">Long thread title that is going to be clipped off</span>
      <MenuItemActions>
        <MenuItemAction @click="() => {}"><Trash /></MenuItemAction>
      </MenuItemActions>
    </MenuItem>
  </MenuContent>
</Menu>
```

### Destructive items
Mark a destructive action (delete, remove, leave) with danger: the label and the highlight take the danger color, and a description keeps its secondary color. MenuLink takes danger too. Put such items last, after a separator.
```vue
<Menu>
  <MenuTrigger>
    <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform aria-label="More actions">
      <DotsHorizontal />
    </Button>
  </MenuTrigger>
  <MenuContent align="end" :min-width="200">
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}"><EditPencil /> Rename</MenuItem>
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}">Duplicate</MenuItem>
    <MenuSeparator />
    <MenuItem @select="() => {}" danger><Trash /> Delete project</MenuItem>
  </MenuContent>
</Menu>
```

## OTP Input
Input for one-time passwords and verification codes, with split digit cells, grouped layouts, error states, and autofill.
Import: import { OTPInput } from "@plexui/vue/components/OTPInput"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/otp-input
Props:
  - length (number) default: 6 — Number of digit cells
  - value / v-model:value (string) — Controlled value; `update:value` fires on every change
  - @change ((value: string) => void) — Fires when the value changes
  - @complete ((value: string) => void) — Fires when all digits are filled
  - grouping (number[]) — Grouping pattern with extra gap (e.g. [3,3])
  - invalid (boolean) default: false — Invalid visual state
  - error-message (string) — Error message below the input
  - disabled (boolean) default: false — Disable all cells
  - auto-focus (boolean) default: false — Auto-focus first cell on mount
  - class (string) — Applied to the root element

### Overview
```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
import { ref } from 'vue';
import { OTPInput } from '@plexui/vue/components/OTPInput';

const value = ref('');
const error = ref(false);
const disabled = ref(false);
</script>

<template>
<OTPInput
  v-model:value="value"
  :invalid="error"
  :error-message="error ? 'Invalid verification code.' : undefined"
  :disabled="disabled"
/>
</template>
```

### Grouped (3+3)
```vue
<OTPInput v-model:value="value" :grouping="[3, 3]" />
```

## Page
"The frame of an app page: header and content share one column, a line comes as the content scrolls, and on a phone the header becomes a floating glass bar."
Import: import { Page, PageActions, PageBar, PageContent, PageFooter, PageHeader, PageHeading, PageLeading, PageSubtitle, PageTitle } from "@plexui/vue/components/Page"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/page
Props:
  - width ("full" | "wide" | "column") default: "full" — Column that header and content share: edge to edge, 1280px or 768px, centred
  - surface ("plain" | "grouped") default: "plain" — Mobile canvas and field-group palette; desktop colors stay unchanged
  - class (string) — Class of the root element
  PageHeader:
    - background ("soft" | "hard" | "glass" | "solid") default: "soft" — How the bar meets the content scrolled under it on a phone, see Background
    - divider (boolean) default: true — Shows a divider once the content has scrolled under the header: on a desktop layout, and on a phone with a hard or glass background
    - class (string) — Class of the band
  PageContent:
    - fill (boolean) default: false — The content fills the page and scrolls inside itself, from 768px up
    - class (string) — Class of the element that scrolls

### Overview
The header has no line at rest. Scroll the content: the line comes after 4px and goes again at the top.
```vue
<Page>
  <PageHeader>
    <PageBar>
      <PageTitle>Orders</PageTitle>
      <PageActions>
        <Button color="primary" pill size="md">New order</Button>
      </PageActions>
    </PageBar>
  </PageHeader>
  <PageContent>...</PageContent>
</Page>
```

### Widths
The width belongs to the page, so the line, the title, the buttons and the content always stand in one column. Take full for tables, wide for overviews and column for settings and forms. For any other width set --page-width on the page. This frame is narrower than a screen, so the preview shows both widths at half their size.
```vue
<Page width="column">
  <PageHeader>...</PageHeader>
  <PageContent>...</PageContent>
</Page>

<Page width="column" style="--page-width: 860px">...</Page>
```

### Heading
PageHeading groups the title with metadata. On a phone the badge and subtitle share a second row below the centered title. The header grows when metadata wraps, and the content keeps the corresponding space below it.
```vue
<PageBar>
  <PageHeading>
    <PageTitle>Menu</PageTitle>
    <PageSubtitle>
      <Badge color="info" size="sm" variant="outline">Draft</Badge>
      <span>Saved a minute ago</span>
    </PageSubtitle>
  </PageHeading>
  <PageActions>...</PageActions>
</PageBar>
```

### Fill
fill stops the page scrolling from 768px up, so a table can scroll inside itself. A table that draws its own line under a sticky head turns the header's off with divider.
```vue
<Page>
  <PageHeader :divider="false">...</PageHeader>
  <PageContent fill>
    <div class="min-h-0 flex-1 overflow-auto">...</div>
  </PageContent>
</Page>
```

### Background
background sets how the header and the footer meet the content scrolled under them on a phone, as a Toolbar does: soft (the default) fades the canvas color out under the bar, hard is an opaque bar with a line, glass a translucent, blurred bar with a hairline, and solid an opaque bar with no line. Hard and glass show themselves once content is under the bar; :divider="false" keeps a header without its line.
```vue
<Page>
  <PageHeader background="hard">...</PageHeader>
  <PageContent>...</PageContent>
  <PageFooter background="hard">
    <Button color="primary" pill block>New order</Button>
  </PageFooter>
</Page>
```

## Page Control
A row of dots, one for each page, the current one solid, that shows where a person is in a flat list of pages and moves between them, as the iOS 26 page control does.
Import: import { PageControl } from "@plexui/vue/components/PageControl"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/page-control
Props:
  - count (number) — Number of pages
  - value (number) — Controlled index of the current page, from 0; bind with v-model:value
  - default-value (number) default: 0 — Initial page (uncontrolled)
  - background ("automatic" | "prominent" | "minimal") default: "automatic" — When the capsule behind the dots shows; minimal also turns off scrubbing
  - value-text ((index: number, count: number) => string) default: Page 2 of 5 — Text assistive technology reads for the current page
  - aria-label (string) default: "Pages" — Name of the page control, when no visible label names it
  - aria-labelledby (string) — The id of the visible label of the page control
  - aria-controls (string) — The id of the element that shows the pages
  - id (string) — The id of the page control

### Overview
Tap the leading or trailing side of the current dot for the previous or next page; with a mouse, click a dot. Drag across the dots to scrub, or past either end to reach the first or last page. Scroll the pages without animation while scrubbing, as details.scrubbing tells.
```vue
<div class="flex w-full max-w-[320px] flex-col items-center gap-3">
  <div
    id="page-control-pages"
    ref="pages"
    class="flex w-full snap-x snap-mandatory overflow-x-auto rounded-2xl bg-[var(--color-surface-secondary)] [scrollbar-width:none]"
    @scroll="page = Math.round($event.target.scrollLeft / $event.target.clientWidth)"
  >
    <div v-for="number in 5" :key="number" class="flex h-40 w-full shrink-0 snap-center items-center justify-center text-2xl font-semibold tabular-nums">
      {{ number }}
    </div>
  </div>
  <PageControl
    :count="5"
    v-model:value="page"
    aria-controls="page-control-pages"
    @change="(index, details) => $refs.pages.scrollTo({ left: index * $refs.pages.clientWidth, behavior: details.scrubbing ? 'instant' : 'smooth' })"
  />
</div>
```

### Many pages
From seven pages the dots past a window around the current page shrink to 6px and 4px, suggesting more pages, and the rest hide, as the kit variants show.
```vue
<div class="flex flex-col items-center gap-2">
  <PageControl :count="12" v-model:value="page" />
  <span class="text-sm text-secondary tabular-nums" aria-hidden="true">Page {{ page + 1 }} of 12</span>
</div>
```

### Background
The capsule behind the dots shows while a pointer presses or scrubs with automatic, always with prominent, for a page control that is the main navigation of the screen, and never with minimal, which also turns off scrubbing since it gives no feedback.
```vue
<div class="grid grid-cols-[auto_auto] items-center gap-x-6 gap-y-4">
  <span class="text-sm text-secondary">Automatic</span>
  <PageControl :count="4" aria-label="Automatic background" />
  <span class="text-sm text-secondary">Prominent</span>
  <PageControl :count="4" aria-label="Prominent background" background="prominent" />
  <span class="text-sm text-secondary">Minimal</span>
  <PageControl :count="4" aria-label="Minimal background" background="minimal" />
</div>
```

## Popover
A floating panel for contextual UI, with flexible sizing, hover triggers, a controllable open state and nesting.
Import: import { Popover, PopoverContent, PopoverTrigger } from "@plexui/vue/components/Popover"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/popover
Props:
  - open / v-model:open (boolean) — Controlled open state; uncontrolled when omitted
  - @update:open ((nextState: boolean) => void) — Emitted when the popover opens or closes
  - show-on-hover (boolean) default: false — Open on hover and focus instead of click
  - hover-open-delay (number) default: 150 — Delay before a hover popover opens (ms)
  PopoverContent:
    - width (number | "auto") default: "auto" — Panel width in px
    - min-width (number | "auto") default: 300 — Minimum panel width in px
    - max-width (number) default: 700 — Maximum panel width in px, capped by the space available in the viewport
    - side ("top" | "right" | "bottom" | "left") default: "bottom" — Preferred placement; flips when it collides
    - align ("start" | "center" | "end") default: "center" — Alignment relative to trigger
    - side-offset (number) default: 8 — Offset from trigger (px)
    - align-offset (number) default: -5 — Offset from the start or end alignment (px)
    - avoid-collisions (boolean) default: true — Reposition to stay inside the viewport and scroll containers
    - collision-padding (number) default: 20 — Space in px to keep from viewport and scroll container boundaries
    - translucent (boolean) default: false — Translucent, blurred surface
    - auto-focus (boolean) default: true — Move focus into the panel when it opens (hover popovers never take focus)
    - data-* (string) — Data attributes land on the panel
    - class (string) — Applied to the panel
    - default slot ({ close, shake }) — Panel content; receives the controller functions

### Overview
```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
import { Button } from '@plexui/vue/components/Button';
import { Popover, PopoverContent, PopoverTrigger } from '@plexui/vue/components/Popover';
import { useBreakpoint } from '@plexui/vue/composables/useBreakpoints';

const wide = useBreakpoint('md');
</script>

<template>
<Popover>
  <PopoverTrigger>
    <Button color="primary" :pill="false">Generate</Button>
  </PopoverTrigger>
  <PopoverContent :side="wide ? 'right' : 'bottom'">
    <div class="p-4">
      <p class="text-sm text-secondary">Popover content. Use with any floating UI.</p>
    </div>
  </PopoverContent>
</Popover>
</template>
```

### Sizing the popover
The popover can be sized with any combination of width, min-width, or max-width. All popovers default to a min-width of 300px, and a max-width of 700px, capped by the space available in the viewport.
```vue
<Popover>
  <PopoverTrigger>
    <Button color="primary" size="sm" variant="ghost" :pill="false" gutter-size="xs" class="gap-1.5">
      <Bolt /> Actions
    </Button>
  </PopoverTrigger>
  <PopoverContent class="flex p-1.5" min-width="auto" side="top">
    <Button color="primary" variant="ghost" :pill="false" size="sm" gutter-size="2xs">
      <Functions :height="18" :width="18" />
    </Button>
    <Button color="primary" variant="ghost" :pill="false" size="sm" gutter-size="2xs">
      <Search :height="18" :width="18" />
    </Button>
    <Button color="primary" variant="ghost" :pill="false" size="sm" gutter-size="2xs">
      <Globe :height="18" :width="18" />
    </Button>
    <Button color="primary" variant="ghost" :pill="false" size="sm" gutter-size="2xs">
      <Code :height="18" :width="18" />
    </Button>
    <Button color="primary" variant="ghost" :pill="false" size="sm" gutter-size="2xs">
      <ImageSquare :height="18" :width="18" />
    </Button>
  </PopoverContent>
</Popover>
```

### Open on hover
Use show-on-hover to create popovers that appear on hover and focus events. This should be used sparingly and with clear intention.By default, hover popovers will not alter the focused element on the page, nor restore focus to the trigger on close. However, if interactive content is present in the popover, and tab is used to navigate into the popover, focus will be restored to the trigger on close.
```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
import { Button } from '@plexui/vue/components/Button';
import { Popover, PopoverContent, PopoverTrigger } from '@plexui/vue/components/Popover';
import { useBreakpoint } from '@plexui/vue/composables/useBreakpoints';

const wide = useBreakpoint('md');
</script>

<template>
<div class="flex flex-col gap-4">
  <Popover show-on-hover>
    <PopoverTrigger>
      <Button color="primary" :pill="false">Static content</Button>
    </PopoverTrigger>
    <PopoverContent :width="320" :side="wide ? 'right' : 'bottom'">
      <div class="p-3">
        <h3 class="mb-0.5 font-semibold">Popover Title</h3>
        <p class="text-sm text-secondary">
          This is an example of a hoverable popover containing presentational content.
        </p>
        <footer class="mt-2 text-xs text-tertiary">Last updated June 20, 2025</footer>
      </div>
    </PopoverContent>
  </Popover>
  <Popover show-on-hover>
    <PopoverTrigger>
      <Button color="primary" :pill="false">Interactive content</Button>
    </PopoverTrigger>
    <PopoverContent :min-width="230" :side="wide ? 'right' : 'bottom'" v-slot="{ close }">
      <div class="p-3">
        <h3 class="mb-0.5 text-sm font-semibold">Popover with actions</h3>
        <p class="text-sm text-secondary">Try using tab to navigate</p>
        <div class="mt-3 flex gap-2">
          <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" :pill="false" @click="close">
            Cancel
          </Button>
          <Button color="primary" :pill="false" @click="close">
            Confirm
          </Button>
        </div>
      </div>
    </PopoverContent>
  </Popover>
</div>
</template>
```

### Using the controller
The default slot of PopoverContent receives {"{ close, shake }"}. Components rendered inside it can get the same two methods from usePopoverController() in their setup(). They do exactly what you think they do.Attempting to submit an empty form calls the shake() methodAfter the simulated two-second request, close() dismisses the popup. Closing or unmounting cancels the pending request; reopening starts a fresh session.
```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
import { onBeforeUnmount, ref } from 'vue';
import { Button } from '@plexui/vue/components/Button';
import { Sparkles } from '@plexui/vue/components/Icon';
import { Popover, PopoverContent, PopoverTrigger } from '@plexui/vue/components/Popover';
import { Textarea } from '@plexui/vue/components/Textarea';
import { useBreakpoint } from '@plexui/vue/composables/useBreakpoints';

const wide = useBreakpoint('md');
const open = ref(false);
const prompt = ref('');
const submitting = ref(false);
let timer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | undefined;
let session = 0;

onBeforeUnmount(() => {
  session++;
  clearTimeout(timer);
});

function setOpen(next: boolean) {
  session++;
  clearTimeout(timer);
  timer = undefined;
  if (next) {
    submitting.value = false;
    prompt.value = '';
  }
  open.value = next;
}

function setPrompt(value: string) {
  prompt.value = value;
}

function submitPrompt(close: () => void, shake: () => void) {
  if (!prompt.value) {
    shake();
    return;
  }
  if (timer !== undefined) return;
  submitting.value = true;
  const currentSession = session;
  timer = setTimeout(() => {
    if (session !== currentSession) return;
    timer = undefined;
    close();
  }, 2000);
}
</script>

<template>
<Popover :open="open" @update:open="setOpen">
  <PopoverTrigger>
    <Button color="primary" :pill="false">Generate</Button>
  </PopoverTrigger>
  <PopoverContent :side="wide ? 'right' : 'bottom'" v-slot="{ close, shake }">
    <form class="pt-2" @submit.prevent="submitPrompt(close, shake)">
      <Textarea
        :value="prompt"
        @update:value="setPrompt"
        aria-label="Prompt"
        variant="ghost"
        gutter-size="xl"
        :rows="3"
        class="w-full"
        placeholder="Describe what you're using the model for, and we'll generate system instructions."
      />
      <div class="px-3 pb-3 flex items-center justify-between">
        <div class="flex gap-1.5 items-center font-normal text-sm text-tertiary">
          <Sparkles :width="16" :height="16" />
          Free beta
        </div>
        <Button color="primary" size="xs" :pill="false" :loading="submitting" type="submit">
          Create
        </Button>
      </div>
    </form>
  </PopoverContent>
</Popover>
</template>
```

### Confirmation
Confirm a destructive action from the button that asks for it. On a desktop the panel opens above the button. On a phone it covers the button, as iOS 26 presents an action sheet from its source: no Cancel button, since a tap anywhere else cancels, the message in the body text and the action as a 48px capsule. Opening moves focus to the panel, not to the action. Copy the shared styles with the example.
```vue
<script setup lang="ts">
import { Button } from '@plexui/vue/components/Button';
import { Popover, PopoverContent, PopoverTrigger } from '@plexui/vue/components/Popover';
import { useBreakpoint } from '@plexui/vue/composables/useBreakpoints';
import styles from './PopoverConfirmation.module.css';

const wide = useBreakpoint('md');
</script>

<template>
<Popover>
  <PopoverTrigger>
    <Button color="danger" variant="soft" :size="wide ? 'md' : '3xl'" :block="!wide" :pill="!wide">
      Delete event
    </Button>
  </PopoverTrigger>
  <PopoverContent side="top" :side-offset="wide ? 8 : -34" :class="styles.Confirmation" v-slot="{ close }">
    <p :class="styles.Message">Are you sure you want to delete this event?</p>
    <Button color="danger" variant="soft" block :pill="!wide" :class="styles.Action" @click="close()">
      Delete event
    </Button>
  </PopoverContent>
</Popover>
</template>
```

## Progress
A horizontal bar that shows task completion, with determinate and indeterminate modes, labelled layouts, and smooth value transitions.
Import: import { Progress } from "@plexui/vue/components/Progress"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/progress
Props:
  - value (number | null) default: null — Current progress between 0 and max. Pass null for indeterminate.
  - max (number) default: 100 — Maximum progress value
  - get-value-label ((value: number, max: number) => string) — Accessible value text (aria-valuetext). Defaults to the rounded percentage, e.g. "67%".
  - class (string) — Applied to the root <div>

### Overview
```vue
<script setup>
import { onMounted, onUnmounted, ref } from "vue"
import { Progress } from "@plexui/vue/components/Progress"

const value = ref(13)
let timer
onMounted(() => {
  timer = setTimeout(() => (value.value = 66), 500)
})
onUnmounted(() => clearTimeout(timer))
</script>

<template>
  <Progress :value="value" aria-label="Loading" />
</template>
```

### With label
Pair a Label with a value readout above the bar, the typical upload or onboarding progress pattern.
```vue
<div style="display: flex; flex-direction: column; gap: 8px; width: 100%; max-width: 320px">
  <div style="display: flex; align-items: baseline; justify-content: space-between; gap: 16px">
    <Label id="progress-upload-label">Uploading plex-ui.zip</Label>
    <span style="color: var(--color-text-tertiary); font-size: 14px">3.2 / 4.8 MB</span>
  </div>
  <Progress aria-labelledby="progress-upload-label" :value="67" />
</div>
```

### Controlled
Bind value to any source, here a Slider through v-model:value.
```vue
<div style="display: flex; width: 100%; max-width: 320px; flex-direction: column; gap: 16px">
  <Progress :value="value" aria-label="Progress" />
  <Slider v-model:value="value" :min="0" :max="100" :step="1" aria-label="Progress value" />
</div>
```

## Progress Steps
Stepper for multi-step flows like checkouts and onboarding, with horizontal or vertical layout, icons, navigation, and color tokens.
Import: import { ProgressSteps, ProgressStepsStep, ProgressStepsTitle, ProgressStepsDescription } from "@plexui/vue/components/ProgressSteps"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/progress-steps
Props:
  - current (number) — Required. Current active step (1-based)
  - orientation ("horizontal" | "vertical") default: "horizontal" — Layout direction
  - size ("sm" | "md" | "lg") default: "md" — Step indicator size
  - color ("default" | "success") default: "default" — Color scheme for completed/active steps
  - connector-style ("solid" | "dashed") default: "solid" — Connector line style
  - variant ("default" | "minimal") default: "default" — "minimal" renders a compact bar (requires total)
  - total (number) — Total steps for minimal variant
  - class (string) — Class applied to the container
  ProgressStepsStep:
    - icon (Component | VNode | string) — Custom icon instead of step number; the icon slot takes precedence

### Overview
```vue
<ProgressSteps
  :current="Number(current)"
  :orientation="orientation"
  :size="size"
  :color="color"
  :connector-style="connectorStyle"
  class="w-full"
>
  <ProgressStepsStep>
    <ProgressStepsTitle>Your details</ProgressStepsTitle>
    <ProgressStepsDescription>Please provide your name and email</ProgressStepsDescription>
  </ProgressStepsStep>
  <ProgressStepsStep>
    <ProgressStepsTitle>Company details</ProgressStepsTitle>
    <ProgressStepsDescription>A few details about your company</ProgressStepsDescription>
  </ProgressStepsStep>
  <ProgressStepsStep>
    <ProgressStepsTitle>Invite your team</ProgressStepsTitle>
    <ProgressStepsDescription>Start collaborating with your team</ProgressStepsDescription>
  </ProgressStepsStep>
  <ProgressStepsStep>
    <ProgressStepsTitle>Add your socials</ProgressStepsTitle>
    <ProgressStepsDescription>Share posts to your social accounts</ProgressStepsDescription>
  </ProgressStepsStep>
</ProgressSteps>
```

### Sizes
Use size to control the scale of step indicators.
```vue
<ProgressSteps :current="Number(current)" :size="size" class="w-full">
  <ProgressStepsStep><ProgressStepsTitle>Step 1</ProgressStepsTitle></ProgressStepsStep>
  <ProgressStepsStep><ProgressStepsTitle>Step 2</ProgressStepsTitle></ProgressStepsStep>
  <ProgressStepsStep><ProgressStepsTitle>Step 3</ProgressStepsTitle></ProgressStepsStep>
  <ProgressStepsStep><ProgressStepsTitle>Step 4</ProgressStepsTitle></ProgressStepsStep>
</ProgressSteps>
```

### Vertical
Use orientation="vertical" to display steps in a vertical layout, ideal for sidebar navigation or multi-step forms. The example also shows the dashed connector style. steps is an array of {"{ title, description }"} objects. On a phone the four columns of a horizontal row cannot hold a step's description, so the Overview and With Icons examples show the steps vertically there; Overview leaves out its orientation control. Progress steps have no iOS control of their own; With Navigation shows a compact composition for a phone screen.
```vue
<div class="flex flex-wrap justify-center gap-20">
  <div>
    <div class="mb-4 text-tertiary text-sm">Default</div>
    <ProgressSteps :current="2" orientation="vertical">
      <ProgressStepsStep v-for="step in steps" :key="step.title">
        <ProgressStepsTitle>{{ step.title }}</ProgressStepsTitle>
        <ProgressStepsDescription>{{ step.description }}</ProgressStepsDescription>
      </ProgressStepsStep>
    </ProgressSteps>
  </div>
  <div>
    <div class="mb-4 text-tertiary text-sm">With dashed connector</div>
    <ProgressSteps :current="3" orientation="vertical" connector-style="dashed">
      <ProgressStepsStep v-for="step in steps" :key="step.title">
        <ProgressStepsTitle>{{ step.title }}</ProgressStepsTitle>
        <ProgressStepsDescription>{{ step.description }}</ProgressStepsDescription>
      </ProgressStepsStep>
    </ProgressSteps>
  </div>
</div>
```

### With Icons
Use the icon slot (or the icon prop) on ProgressStepsStep to display custom icons instead of step numbers.
```vue
<ProgressSteps :current="3" class="w-full">
  <ProgressStepsStep>
    <template #icon><Email /></template>
    <ProgressStepsTitle>Your details</ProgressStepsTitle>
    <ProgressStepsDescription>Please provide your name and email</ProgressStepsDescription>
  </ProgressStepsStep>
  <ProgressStepsStep>
    <template #icon><Globe /></template>
    <ProgressStepsTitle>Company details</ProgressStepsTitle>
    <ProgressStepsDescription>Website and location</ProgressStepsDescription>
  </ProgressStepsStep>
  <ProgressStepsStep>
    <template #icon><Users /></template>
    <ProgressStepsTitle>Invite your team</ProgressStepsTitle>
    <ProgressStepsDescription>Start collaborating</ProgressStepsDescription>
  </ProgressStepsStep>
  <ProgressStepsStep>
    <template #icon><Bolt /></template>
    <ProgressStepsTitle>Get started</ProgressStepsTitle>
    <ProgressStepsDescription>Ready to go</ProgressStepsDescription>
  </ProgressStepsStep>
</ProgressSteps>
```

### With Navigation
Example of integrating with navigation controls.
```vue
<div class="flex flex-col items-center gap-6">
  <ProgressSteps variant="minimal" :current="step" :total="4" />
  <div class="text-center">
    <h3 class="text-lg font-semibold">Step {{ step }} of 4</h3>
    <p class="text-secondary mt-1">{{ steps[Math.min(step - 1, 3)].title }}</p>
  </div>
  <div class="flex gap-3">
    <Button color="secondary" variant="outline" class="w-28" :disabled="step === 1" @click="step = Math.max(1, step - 1)">
      Previous
    </Button>
    <Button color="primary" class="w-28" :disabled="step === 4" @click="step = Math.min(4, step + 1)">
      Next
    </Button>
  </div>
</div>
```

### Minimal
Use variant="minimal" for a compact horizontal bar indicator. Requires total prop.
```vue
<ProgressSteps variant="minimal" :current="Number(current)" :total="4" :color="color" />
```

### Icons Only
Omit ProgressStepsTitle and ProgressStepsDescription for a minimal indicator when step details are shown elsewhere.
```vue
<ProgressSteps :current="2" class="w-full">
  <ProgressStepsStep />
  <ProgressStepsStep />
  <ProgressStepsStep />
  <ProgressStepsStep />
</ProgressSteps>
```

### Colors
Use color to change the color scheme of completed and active steps.
```vue
<div class="flex flex-col gap-12 w-full">
  <div>
    <div class="mb-4 text-tertiary text-sm">Default</div>
    <ProgressSteps :current="3" class="w-full">
      <ProgressStepsStep><ProgressStepsTitle>Step 1</ProgressStepsTitle></ProgressStepsStep>
      <ProgressStepsStep><ProgressStepsTitle>Step 2</ProgressStepsTitle></ProgressStepsStep>
      <ProgressStepsStep><ProgressStepsTitle>Step 3</ProgressStepsTitle></ProgressStepsStep>
      <ProgressStepsStep><ProgressStepsTitle>Step 4</ProgressStepsTitle></ProgressStepsStep>
    </ProgressSteps>
  </div>
  <div>
    <div class="mb-4 text-tertiary text-sm">Success</div>
    <ProgressSteps :current="3" color="success" class="w-full">
      <ProgressStepsStep><ProgressStepsTitle>Step 1</ProgressStepsTitle></ProgressStepsStep>
      <ProgressStepsStep><ProgressStepsTitle>Step 2</ProgressStepsTitle></ProgressStepsStep>
      <ProgressStepsStep><ProgressStepsTitle>Step 3</ProgressStepsTitle></ProgressStepsStep>
      <ProgressStepsStep><ProgressStepsTitle>Step 4</ProgressStepsTitle></ProgressStepsStep>
    </ProgressSteps>
  </div>
</div>
```

## Radio Group
Radio group for single-choice selection with row or column layout, right-aligned indicators, rich item content, and card-style options.
Import: import { RadioGroup, RadioGroupItem } from "@plexui/vue/components/RadioGroup"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/radio-group
Props:
  - value (string) — Selected value. Bind with v-model:value; leave unset for an uncontrolled group
  - default-value (string) — Initial selected value (uncontrolled)
  - direction ("col" | "row") default: "row" — Item flow direction
  - disabled (boolean) default: false — Disable the whole group
  - name (string) — Name of the hidden radio inputs submitted with the owning form
  - required (boolean) default: false — Marks the group and every hidden radio input as required
  - aria-label (string) — Accessible group label
  - class (string) — Extra classes on the group element
  - default slot (VNode[]) — RadioGroupItem children
  - @update:value ((value: string) => void) — Emitted when the selected option changes; powers v-model:value
  - @change ((value: string) => void) — Emitted with update:value, named after the React onChange prop
  RadioGroupItem:
    - value (string) — Item value
    - disabled (boolean) default: false — Disable this item only
    - required (boolean) — Marks this item as required
    - block (boolean) default: false — Stretch item to fill available width
    - orientation ("left" | "right") default: "left" — Position of the radio indicator relative to the label
    - description (string) — Second line below the option's label, linked via aria-describedby (or the description slot)
    - class (string) — Extra classes on the label element
    - default slot (VNode[]) — Label content (text or rich content)

### Overview
```vue
<div>
  <h3 class="font-semibold text-sm mb-3">Notification frequency</h3>
  <RadioGroup direction="col" v-model:value="frequency" aria-label="Notification frequency">
    <RadioGroupItem value="daily">Daily</RadioGroupItem>
    <RadioGroupItem value="weekly">Weekly</RadioGroupItem>
    <RadioGroupItem value="monthly">Monthly</RadioGroupItem>
    <RadioGroupItem value="never">Never</RadioGroupItem>
  </RadioGroup>
</div>
```

### With Checkbox
Place RadioGroup alongside Checkbox controls to verify that indicator sizes, label baselines, and spacing align consistently in both horizontal and vertical layouts.
```vue
<div class="flex flex-col gap-6 w-full max-w-md">
  <Field label="Notification channel" description="Choose how you'd like to be notified." v-slot="{ id }">
    <RadioGroup :direction="direction" default-value="email" aria-label="Notification channel">
      <RadioGroupItem
        v-for="channel in [
          { value: 'email', label: 'Email', description: 'Messages delivered to your inbox.' },
          { value: 'sms', label: 'SMS', description: 'Text messages to your phone number.' },
          { value: 'push', label: 'Push', description: 'Real-time alerts on your device.' },
        ]"
        :key="channel.value"
        :value="channel.value"
      >
        <div v-if="description === 'true'" class="flex flex-col">
          <span class="font-medium">{{ channel.label }}</span>
          <span class="text-xs text-secondary mt-0.5">{{ channel.description }}</span>
        </div>
        <template v-else>{{ channel.label }}</template>
      </RadioGroupItem>
    </RadioGroup>
  </Field>
  <Field label="Topics" description="Select the topics you want to follow." v-slot="{ id }">
    <div :class="direction === 'row' ? 'flex flex-row gap-5' : 'flex flex-col gap-2.5'">
      <Checkbox
        v-for="topic in [
          { label: 'Product updates', description: 'New features and improvements.', checked: true },
          { label: 'Security alerts', description: 'Account activity and login notifications.', checked: true },
          { label: 'Community', description: 'Events, meetups, and discussions.', checked: false },
        ]"
        :key="topic.label"
        :default-checked="topic.checked"
      >
        <template #label>
          <div v-if="description === 'true'" class="flex flex-col">
            <span class="font-medium">{{ topic.label }}</span>
            <span class="text-xs text-secondary mt-0.5">{{ topic.description }}</span>
          </div>
          <template v-else>{{ topic.label }}</template>
        </template>
      </Checkbox>
    </div>
  </Field>
</div>
```

### Descriptions
Combine Field-level label and description with item-level descriptions for options that need additional context. Use a plain label without descriptions when the options are self-explanatory.
```vue
<div class="flex flex-col max-w-sm w-full divide-y divide-alpha/15">
  <div class="pb-5">
    <Field label="Notification channel" description="Choose how you'd like to be notified." v-slot="{ id }">
      <RadioGroup direction="col" v-model:value="channel" aria-label="Notification channel">
        <RadioGroupItem value="email">
          <div class="flex flex-col">
            <span class="font-medium">Email</span>
            <span class="text-xs text-secondary mt-0.5">Messages delivered to your inbox.</span>
          </div>
        </RadioGroupItem>
        <RadioGroupItem value="sms">
          <div class="flex flex-col">
            <span class="font-medium">SMS</span>
            <span class="text-xs text-secondary mt-0.5">Text messages to your phone number.</span>
          </div>
        </RadioGroupItem>
        <RadioGroupItem value="push">
          <div class="flex flex-col">
            <span class="font-medium">Push</span>
            <span class="text-xs text-secondary mt-0.5">Real-time alerts on your device.</span>
          </div>
        </RadioGroupItem>
      </RadioGroup>
    </Field>
  </div>
  <div class="pt-5">
    <Field label="Notification frequency" v-slot="{ id }">
      <RadioGroup direction="col" v-model:value="frequency" aria-label="Notification frequency">
        <RadioGroupItem value="daily">Daily</RadioGroupItem>
        <RadioGroupItem value="weekly">Weekly</RadioGroupItem>
        <RadioGroupItem value="monthly">Monthly</RadioGroupItem>
        <RadioGroupItem value="never">Never</RadioGroupItem>
      </RadioGroup>
    </Field>
  </div>
</div>
```

### Direction
Use direction to control whether the items flow in a column or row. An optimal gap will be dynamically applied based on the orientation.
```vue
<RadioGroup :direction="direction" default-value="light" aria-label="Theme">
  <RadioGroupItem value="light">Light</RadioGroupItem>
  <RadioGroupItem value="dark">Dark</RadioGroupItem>
  <RadioGroupItem value="system">System</RadioGroupItem>
</RadioGroup>
```

### Orientation
Use orientation="right" on RadioGroupItem to place the radio indicator after the label text. Useful for settings lists and card-style selections where the content should lead.
```vue
<RadioGroup class="w-56" direction="col" default-value="email" aria-label="Default notification channel">
  <RadioGroupItem value="email" :orientation="orientation" block>Email</RadioGroupItem>
  <RadioGroupItem value="sms" :orientation="orientation" block>SMS</RadioGroupItem>
  <RadioGroupItem value="push" :orientation="orientation" block>Push notification</RadioGroupItem>
</RadioGroup>
```

### Group disabled
Use disabled on RadioGroup to disable all radio options.
```vue
<RadioGroup :disabled="disabled" default-value="standard" aria-label="Shipping method">
  <RadioGroupItem value="standard">Standard</RadioGroupItem>
  <RadioGroupItem value="express">Express</RadioGroupItem>
  <RadioGroupItem value="overnight">Overnight</RadioGroupItem>
</RadioGroup>
```

### Item disabled
Use disabled on a single RadioGroupItem to disable only that option.
```vue
<RadioGroup aria-label="Delivery method" class="max-w-[320px] m-auto" direction="col">
  <RadioGroupItem value="standard">Standard shipping (3-5 days)</RadioGroupItem>
  <RadioGroupItem value="express" :disabled="disabled">Express shipping (1-2 days)</RadioGroupItem>
  <RadioGroupItem value="same-day">
    Same-day delivery, available in select metro areas with a minimum order of $50
  </RadioGroupItem>
</RadioGroup>
```

### With description
Use description (or the description slot) on an item for a second line under its label. It sits outside the label, so the radio keeps the label as its name and gets the description through aria-describedby; clicking it does not select the option.
```vue
<RadioGroup direction="col" v-model:value="value" aria-label="Spacing">
  <RadioGroupItem value="default" description="Standard spacing for most use cases.">Default</RadioGroupItem>
  <RadioGroupItem value="comfortable" description="More space between elements.">Comfortable</RadioGroupItem>
  <RadioGroupItem value="compact" description="Minimal spacing for dense layouts.">Compact</RadioGroupItem>
</RadioGroup>
```

### With Field
Wrap a RadioGroup in a Field for a shared label and description. Bind the field props from the slot onto the group so it is described by the Field's description (aria-describedby).
```vue
<div class="max-w-sm">
  <Field label="Notification method" description="Choose how you want to receive notifications." v-slot="fieldProps">
    <RadioGroup direction="col" v-model:value="value" aria-label="Notification method" v-bind="fieldProps">
      <RadioGroupItem value="email">Email</RadioGroupItem>
      <RadioGroupItem value="sms">SMS</RadioGroupItem>
      <RadioGroupItem value="push">Push notification</RadioGroupItem>
    </RadioGroup>
  </Field>
</div>
```

### With Field error
When a Field has an error-message, it sets aria-invalid on the child control for screen readers. The error message is displayed below the group.
```vue
<div class="max-w-sm">
  <Field
    label="Subscription plan"
    description="Select a plan to continue."
    :error-message="showError ? 'Please select a subscription plan.' : undefined"
    v-slot="fieldProps"
  >
    <RadioGroup direction="col" v-model:value="value" aria-label="Subscription plan" v-bind="fieldProps">
      <RadioGroupItem value="monthly">Monthly ($9.99/month)</RadioGroupItem>
      <RadioGroupItem value="yearly">Yearly ($99.99/year)</RadioGroupItem>
      <RadioGroupItem value="lifetime">Lifetime ($299.99)</RadioGroupItem>
    </RadioGroup>
  </Field>
</div>
```

### Choice card
Style each radio option as a bordered card with visual selection state. The selected card gets a highlighted border using design tokens. Use orientation="right" to place the indicator after the content. Wrap each RadioGroupItem in a card container with border and borderRadius on the same element.
```vue
<RadioGroup class="flex-col w-[320px] gap-3" v-model:value="value" aria-label="Workspace type">
  <div
    v-for="card in [
      { id: 'personal', title: 'Personal', description: 'For hobby projects and experimentation.' },
      { id: 'team', title: 'Team', description: 'Collaborate with up to 10 members.' },
      { id: 'business', title: 'Business', description: 'SSO, audit logs, and priority support.' },
    ]"
    :key="card.id"
    :style="{
      border: '1px solid',
      borderColor: value === card.id ? 'var(--color-background-primary-solid)' : 'var(--color-border-primary-outline)',
      borderRadius: 'var(--radius-lg)',
      backgroundColor: value === card.id ? 'var(--color-background-primary-soft-alpha)' : undefined,
      transition: 'border-color 150ms ease, background-color 150ms ease',
    }"
  >
    <RadioGroupItem :value="card.id" block :orientation="orientation" class="gap-3 p-3">
      <div class="flex flex-col flex-1">
        <span class="font-semibold">{{ card.title }}</span>
        <span class="text-xs text-secondary mt-0.5">{{ card.description }}</span>
      </div>
    </RadioGroupItem>
  </div>
</RadioGroup>
```

### Card selection
Leverage component composition and class overrides to build card-style radio selections with rich content per option.
```vue
<RadioGroup class="flex-col w-full min-w-0 max-w-[390px] gap-4" aria-label="Sample options">
  <RadioGroupItem class="gap-2.5" value="basic">
    <div>
      <h4 class="font-semibold mb-1">Basic Plan</h4>
      <p class="text-secondary text-sm">5 GB storage • Email support • Free</p>
    </div>
  </RadioGroupItem>
  <hr class="border-default" />
  <RadioGroupItem class="gap-2.5" value="standard">
    <div>
      <h4 class="font-semibold mb-1">Standard Plan</h4>
      <p class="text-secondary text-sm">100 GB storage • Priority email support • $9.99/m</p>
    </div>
  </RadioGroupItem>
  <hr class="border-default" />
  <Tooltip :max-width="258" interactive>
    <template #content>
      Contact our <TextLink href="#">support team</TextLink> for more information on enterprise plans.
    </template>
    <TooltipTrigger>
      <RadioGroupItem class="gap-2.5" value="enterprise" disabled>
        <div>
          <h4 class="font-semibold mb-1">Enterprise Plan</h4>
          <p class="text-secondary text-sm">Unlimited storage • 24/7 phone support • Custom pricing</p>
        </div>
      </RadioGroupItem>
    </TooltipTrigger>
  </Tooltip>
</RadioGroup>
```

### Grouped sections
Use dividers to separate radio groups into logical sections. Each section can be wrapped in a Field with its own label and description.
```vue
<Page surface="grouped" class="h-dvh" data-form-screen style="--page-content-padding-top:10px; --page-content-padding-bottom:16px; --page-footer-gutter:24px; --page-footer-padding-bottom:max(24px, env(safe-area-inset-bottom, 0px))">
  <Toolbar>
    <ToolbarLeading><DemoBackButton /></ToolbarLeading>
    <ToolbarTitle>Preferences</ToolbarTitle>
  </Toolbar>
  <PageContent data-form-content>
    <form ref="screenForm" class="flex flex-col gap-6" @submit.prevent="" >
      <RadioGroup direction="col" class="w-full" v-model:value="visibility" aria-label="Visibility">
        <FieldGroup title="Visibility" description="Control who can see project resources.">
          <Field row label="Hidden"><RadioGroupItem value="hidden" /></Field>
          <Field row label="Visible to organization owners"><RadioGroupItem value="owners" /></Field>
          <Field row label="Visible to everyone"><RadioGroupItem value="everyone" /></Field>
        </FieldGroup>
      </RadioGroup>
      <RadioGroup direction="col" class="w-full" v-model:value="frequency" aria-label="Digest frequency">
        <FieldGroup title="Digest frequency" description="How often you receive summary emails.">
          <Field row label="Daily"><RadioGroupItem value="daily" /></Field>
          <Field row label="Weekly"><RadioGroupItem value="weekly" /></Field>
          <Field row label="Monthly"><RadioGroupItem value="monthly" /></Field>
          <Field row label="Never"><RadioGroupItem value="never" /></Field>
        </FieldGroup>
      </RadioGroup>
    </form>
  </PageContent>

</Page>
```

## Select
Select dropdown with grouped options, per-item descriptions, multiple selection, action items, and column-based layouts.
Import: import { Select } from "@plexui/vue/components/Select"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/select
Props:
  - options (Options<T>) — Array of options or grouped options
  - v-model:value (string | string[]) — Selected value (single) or values (multiple)
  - @change ((option | options) => void) — Called when selection changes, with the selected option (or options with multiple)
  - multiple (boolean) default: false — Allow multiple selection. value, @change and @update:value are typed by it
  - placeholder (string) default: "Select..." — Trigger placeholder
  - variant ("soft" | "outline" | "ghost") default: "outline" — Trigger variant
  - size (ControlSize) default: "md" — Trigger size (3xs to 3xl)
  - pill (boolean) default: false — Rounded trigger
  - clearable (boolean) default: false — Show clear button
  - block (boolean) default: variant !== "ghost" — Full width trigger
  - disabled (boolean) default: false — Disable select. A disabled select is not submitted or validated with its form
  - loading (boolean) default: false — Loading state; shows loading-placeholder and keeps the menu closed
  - loading-placeholder (string) default: "Loading..." — Trigger text while loading
  - all-selected-label (string) default: "All selected" — Text of a multiple trigger when every option is selected
  - name (string) — Name of the hidden form input. With multiple, every selected value is submitted
  - required (boolean) — Required for native form validation
  - id (string) — Id of the hidden form input
  - actions (array) — Actions below options list
  - check-position ("start" | "end" | "none") default: "start" — Where the check indicator sits in the list. "none" removes the indicator column for lists that never carry a selection
  - #optionView / OptionView (slot (option) | Component) — Custom option render
  - #triggerView / TriggerView (slot | Component) — Custom trigger render: the selected option, or { values, selectedAll } with multiple
  - TriggerStartIcon (Component) — Icon at the start of the trigger
  - #trigger / trigger (slot ({ open, onToggle }) | function) — Custom trigger element, wrapped as the popover trigger
  - dropdown-icon-type ("chevronDown" | "dropdown" | "none") default: "dropdown" — Indicator at the end of the trigger
  - side ("top" | "bottom") default: "bottom" — Preferred side of the list
  - align ("start" | "center" | "end") default: "center" when block, otherwise "start" — Alignment of the list against the trigger
  - list-width (number | "auto") default: trigger width (fits the options when not block) — Width of the list in px
  - list-min-width (number | "auto") default: trigger width — Minimum width of the list in px
  - list-max-width (number | "auto") default: "auto" (300 when not block) — Maximum width of the list in px
  - search-placeholder (string) — Search input placeholder
  - search-aria-label (string) default: "Search options" — Accessible name of the search input, independent of the trigger label
  - #searchEmptyMessage / search-empty-message (slot | string) default: "No results found." — Shown when the search matches nothing
  - search-size (ControlSize) default: "md" — Size of the search input in the menu, such as 2xl for 44px touch targets on phones
  - search-predicate ((option, term) => boolean) — Filters options while searching
  - aria-label / aria-labelledby (string) — Accessible name of the trigger and the list

### Overview
Bind the selection with v-model:value: a string, or an array of strings with multiple. The @change event carries the selected option (or options), like React's onChange.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<div style="width: 200px">
  <Select
    v-if="multiple"
    key="multi"
    placeholder="Select fruits..."
    :options="fruitsOptions"
    name="fruits"
    multiple
    :variant="variant"
    :size="size"
    :clearable="clearable"
    :check-position="checkPosition"
    v-model:value="multiFruits"
  >
    <template #triggerView="{ values, selectedAll }">{{ selectedAll ? "All fruits" : values.length === 1 ? values[0].label : values.length + " fruits" }}</template>
  </Select>
  <Select
    v-else
    key="single"
    placeholder="Select a fruit..."
    :options="fruitsOptions"
    name="fruits"
    :multiple="false"
    :variant="variant"
    :size="size"
    :clearable="clearable"
    :check-position="checkPosition"
    v-model:value="fruit"
  />
</div>

<!-- Phone: the select is the value of a row. -->
<FieldGroup>
  <Field row :label="multiple ? 'Fruits' : 'Fruit'">
    <Select
      v-if="multiple"
      key="multi"
      placeholder="None"
      :options="fruitsOptions"
      name="fruits"
      multiple
      :block="false"
      align="end"
      :clearable="clearable"
      :check-position="checkPosition"
      v-model:value="multiFruits"
    >
      <template #triggerView="{ values, selectedAll }">{{ selectedAll ? "All fruits" : values.length === 1 ? values[0].label : values.length + " fruits" }}</template>
    </Select>
    <Select
      v-else
      key="single"
      placeholder="None"
      :options="fruitsOptions"
      name="fruits"
      :multiple="false"
      :block="false"
      align="end"
      :clearable="clearable"
      :check-position="checkPosition"
      v-model:value="fruit"
    />
  </Field>
</FieldGroup>
```

### With descriptions
Options carrying a description field render a secondary line under each label. A description is a string, a VNode or a render function; here each role's is a render function returning {''}. The title stays semibold in every state (idle, highlighted, selected): the component handles that automatically, no option-class-name needed. Pass a component to TriggerStartIcon to reflect the selected option's identity in the trigger. In a phone row the value stands alone at the row's end, so the row has no trigger icon.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<div style="width: 220px">
  <Select
    v-model:value="role"
    :options="roles"
    placeholder="Select role..."
    align="start"
    :list-min-width="260"
    :variant="variant"
    :size="size"
    :check-position="checkPosition"
    :TriggerStartIcon="triggerIcon ? (role === 'owner' ? UserLock : User) : undefined"
  />
</div>

<!-- Phone -->
<FieldGroup>
  <Field row label="Role">
    <Select
      v-model:value="role"
      :options="roles"
      :block="false"
      align="end"
      :list-min-width="260"
      :check-position="checkPosition"
    />
  </Field>
</FieldGroup>
```

### Actions
Custom, arbitrary actions can be displayed below the options list via the actions prop. Each action has an id, a label, an optional Icon component and className, and an onSelect callback.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<div style="width: 150px">
  <Select
    variant="soft"
    v-model:value="value"
    block
    :options="items"
    placeholder="Select..."
    align="start"
    :list-min-width="240"
    :actions="[
      { id: 'create', label: 'Create project', Icon: Plus, onSelect: () => {} },
      { id: 'overview', label: 'Organization overview', Icon: Workspace, onSelect: () => {} },
    ]"
  />
</div>

<!-- Phone -->
<FieldGroup>
  <Field row label="Project">
    <Select
      v-model:value="value"
      :block="false"
      :options="items"
      placeholder="None"
      align="end"
      :list-min-width="240"
      :actions="[
        { id: 'create', label: 'Create project', Icon: Plus, onSelect: () => {} },
        { id: 'overview', label: 'Organization overview', Icon: Workspace, onSelect: () => {} },
      ]"
    />
  </Field>
</FieldGroup>
```

### Grouped options
Select supports option groups, similar to the native &lt;optgroup&gt; element, and per-option tooltips. Each group is announced as a group named by its label. All selects are searchable by default, but will only present a searchbox when it makes sense. Currently, this means when there are more than 15 options.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<div style="width: 200px">
  <Select
    variant="outline"
    v-model:value="value"
    side="bottom"
    size="lg"
    :options="groupedItems"
    :list-min-width="300"
    search-placeholder="Select an option..."
    clearable
  />
</div>

<!-- Phone -->
<FieldGroup>
  <Field row label="Plan">
    <Select
      v-model:value="value"
      :block="false"
      align="end"
      :options="groupedItems"
      :list-min-width="300"
      search-placeholder="Select an option..."
    />
  </Field>
</FieldGroup>
```

### Multiple selection
Multiple selection is supported through the multiple property. This changes how v-model:value, @change, and the #triggerView slot are typed, accepting and returning arrays of values.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<div style="width: 200px">
  <Select
    variant="soft"
    placeholder="Select fruits..."
    :options="fruitsOptions"
    name="fruits"
    multiple
    clearable
    v-model:value="fruits"
  >
    <template #triggerView="{ values, selectedAll }">{{ selectedAll ? "All fruits" : values.length === 1 ? values[0].label : values.length + " fruits" }}</template>
  </Select>
</div>

<!-- Phone -->
<FieldGroup>
  <Field row label="Fruits">
    <Select
      placeholder="None"
      :options="fruitsOptions"
      name="fruits"
      multiple
      clearable
      :block="false"
      align="end"
      v-model:value="fruits"
    >
      <template #triggerView="{ values, selectedAll }">{{ selectedAll ? "All fruits" : values.length === 1 ? values[0].label : values.length + " fruits" }}</template>
    </Select>
  </Field>
</FieldGroup>
```

### Column configuration
A column-visibility panel built with Popover and Switch. Toggling a switch moves the column between shown and hidden groups. On a phone the panel is a sheet (Dialog with mobile-variant="sheet") of Field rows with switches, in a fixed order: as in iOS settings, a row stays in place when its switch changes.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<Popover>
  <PopoverTrigger>
    <Button color="secondary" variant="outline" size="sm"><SettingsSlider :width="15" :height="15" /> Columns</Button>
  </PopoverTrigger>
  <PopoverContent :min-width="240" side="bottom" align="start">
    <div class="py-1.5">
      <div v-if="shown.length > 0">
        <div
          v-for="col in allColumns.filter((c) => shown.includes(c.id))"
          :key="col.id"
          class="flex items-center justify-between gap-4 px-3 py-1.5 cursor-pointer hover:bg-alpha/5"
          @click="shown = shown.filter((c) => c !== col.id)"
        >
          <span class="text-sm">{{ col.label }}</span>
          <Switch :checked="true" :aria-label="col.label" />
        </div>
      </div>
      <div v-if="shown.length < allColumns.length">
        <div
          v-for="col in allColumns.filter((c) => !shown.includes(c.id))"
          :key="col.id"
          class="flex items-center justify-between gap-4 px-3 py-1.5 cursor-pointer hover:bg-alpha/5"
          @click="shown = [...shown, col.id]"
        >
          <span class="text-sm text-tertiary">{{ col.label }}</span>
          <Switch :checked="false" :aria-label="col.label" />
        </div>
      </div>
    </div>
  </PopoverContent>
</Popover>

<!-- Phone: a sheet of switch rows in a fixed order. -->
<Dialog>
  <DialogTrigger>
    <Button color="secondary" variant="outline" size="sm"><SettingsSlider :width="15" :height="15" /> Columns</Button>
  </DialogTrigger>
  <DialogContent mobile-variant="sheet" @open-auto-focus="$event.preventDefault(); $refs.heading.focus({ preventScroll: true })">
    <DialogHeader>
      <div ref="heading" tabindex="-1" class="outline-none"><DialogTitle>Columns</DialogTitle></div>
    </DialogHeader>
    <DialogBody>
      <FieldGroup>
        <Field v-for="col in allColumns" :key="col.id" row :label="col.label">
          <Switch color="accent" :checked="shown.includes(col.id)" @update:checked="(on) => (shown = on ? [...shown, col.id] : shown.filter((c) => c !== col.id))" />
        </Field>
      </FieldGroup>
    </DialogBody>
  </DialogContent>
</Dialog>
```

## Select Control
Select control primitive for building custom dropdowns, with variants, sizing, start and dropdown icons, and clearable, loading, and invalid states.
Import: import { SelectControl } from "@plexui/vue/components/SelectControl"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/select-control
Props:
  - selected (boolean) default: false — Whether a value is selected (affects placeholder styling)
  - variant ("soft" | "outline" | "ghost") default: "outline" — Visual variant
  - size (ControlSize) default: "md" — Control size (3xs to 3xl)
  - pill (boolean) default: false — Fully rounded trigger
  - StartIcon (Component) — Icon at start
  - @clear-click (() => void) — Enables the clear button when set (a function prop, onClearClick)
  - interact-on-enter (boolean) default: false — Enter interacts too, as on a combobox button. Off by default, as Enter may be an attempt to submit a form
  - @interact (() => void) — Called on pointer down, ArrowDown, ArrowUp and Space (a function prop, onInteract). Without it, a wrapping trigger opens on pointer down
  - dropdown-icon-type ("chevronDown" | "dropdown" | "none") default: "dropdown" — Dropdown indicator style
  - invalid (boolean) default: false — Invalid state
  - disabled (boolean) default: false — Disable control
  - block (boolean) default: false — Full width
  - loading (boolean) default: false — Loading state (aria-busy); hides the indicators and ignores interaction
  - optically-align ("start" | "end") — Optical alignment
  - class (string) — Applied to the control

### Overview
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<Menu>
  <MenuTrigger>
    <SelectControl class="w-[200px]" :selected="!!value" @clear-click="value = ''" :StartIcon="CalendarAlt">{{ value || "Select date..." }}</SelectControl>
  </MenuTrigger>
  <MenuContent :min-width="200" :width="200">
    <MenuRadioGroup v-model:value="value">
      <MenuRadioItem value="Today">Today</MenuRadioItem>
      <MenuRadioItem value="Last week">Last week</MenuRadioItem>
      <MenuRadioItem value="Last month">Last month</MenuRadioItem>
      <MenuRadioItem value="Last 3 months">Last 3 months</MenuRadioItem>
    </MenuRadioGroup>
  </MenuContent>
</Menu>

<!-- Phone -->
<FieldGroup>
  <Field row label="Period">
    <template #default="control">
      <Menu>
        <MenuTrigger>
          <SelectControl v-bind="control" :selected="!!value">{{ value || "None" }}</SelectControl>
        </MenuTrigger>
        <MenuContent :min-width="200" align="end">
          <MenuRadioGroup v-model:value="value">
            <MenuRadioItem value="">None</MenuRadioItem>
            <MenuRadioItem value="Today">Today</MenuRadioItem>
            <MenuRadioItem value="Last week">Last week</MenuRadioItem>
            <MenuRadioItem value="Last month">Last month</MenuRadioItem>
            <MenuRadioItem value="Last 3 months">Last 3 months</MenuRadioItem>
          </MenuRadioGroup>
        </MenuContent>
      </Menu>
    </template>
  </Field>
</FieldGroup>
```

### Variants
Use the variant prop to stylize the select control. Available variant styles are similar to {""}, but aligned with subtle differences from {""} variant treatments. In a phone row the row is the frame, so this example is desktop only; Invalid and Disabled show the row's states.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<div class="w-full min-w-0 max-w-[400px]">
  <div class="flex flex-col gap-6">
    <div v-for="variant in ['soft', 'outline', 'ghost']" :key="variant" class="flex items-center">
      <div class="text-right text-sm mr-8 min-w-[4rem]" style="color: var(--color-text-tertiary)">{{ variant }}</div>
      <div class="min-w-0 flex-1">
        <SelectControl
          class="w-full"
          :variant="variant"
          selected
          :StartIcon="Cabinet"
          @clear-click="() => {}"
          :invalid="invalid"
          :disabled="disabled"
        >Sample value</SelectControl>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>
</div>

<!-- Phone -->
<FieldGroup>
  <Field row label="Folder" :error-message="invalid ? 'Choose a folder you can edit.' : undefined">
    <SelectControl selected :invalid="invalid" :disabled="disabled">Sample value</SelectControl>
  </Field>
</FieldGroup>
```

### Sizing & roundness
Sizing of the control is determined with the size prop. Each size provides dynamic values for various aspects of the control, including icon and indicator placement, as well font-size and gutter values.Create fully rounded controls with the pill prop. When pill is set, extra horizontal gutter is dynamically applied, and the clear action is also set to pill. On Phone the row sets the control's size, so a row's control takes no size or pill, and this example is desktop only.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<div class="flex flex-col gap-2 justify-start items-start w-[240px]">
  <SelectControl class="w-[200px]" :size="size" :pill="pill" :variant="variant" dropdown-icon-type="none">Select...</SelectControl>
  <SelectControl class="w-[200px]" :size="size" :pill="pill" :variant="variant" loading>Select...</SelectControl>
  <SelectControl class="w-[200px]" :size="size" :pill="pill" :variant="variant">Select...</SelectControl>
  <SelectControl class="w-[200px]" :size="size" :pill="pill" :variant="variant" :StartIcon="Tools" dropdown-icon-type="none">Tool calls</SelectControl>
  <SelectControl class="w-[200px]" :size="size" :pill="pill" :variant="variant" @clear-click="() => {}" selected :StartIcon="Tools" dropdown-icon-type="none">With tool calls</SelectControl>
  <SelectControl class="w-[200px]" :size="size" :pill="pill" :variant="variant" @clear-click="() => {}" selected :StartIcon="CalendarAlt" dropdown-icon-type="chevronDown">Today</SelectControl>
  <SelectControl class="w-[200px]" :size="size" :pill="pill" :variant="variant" @clear-click="() => {}" selected :StartIcon="CalendarAlt">Today</SelectControl>
</div>

<!-- Phone -->
<FieldGroup>
  <Field row label="Model">
    <SelectControl>None</SelectControl>
  </Field>
  <Field row label="Tools">
    <SelectControl loading>Loading...</SelectControl>
  </Field>
  <Field row label="Date">
    <SelectControl selected>Today</SelectControl>
  </Field>
</FieldGroup>
```

### Selected
Use selected to indicate if the control has a selected value, which toggles visual placeholder styling.As you'll notice here, this doesn't do anything to the content of the control. That's up to you. In a phone row a selected value takes the secondary label color (the primary one in a group with columns), a placeholder the tertiary one.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<SelectControl class="w-[200px]" :selected="selected">Select...</SelectControl>

<!-- Phone -->
<FieldGroup>
  <Field row label="Fruit">
    <SelectControl :selected="selected">Mango</SelectControl>
  </Field>
</FieldGroup>
```

### Start icon
Pass a component to StartIcon to display an icon in the control. Sizing, spacing, and coloring are handled automatically. In a phone row the value stands alone at the row's end, so the row's control has no start icon, and this example is desktop only.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<SelectControl class="w-[200px]" :StartIcon="CalendarAlt">Select date...</SelectControl>

<!-- Phone -->
<FieldGroup>
  <Field row label="Date">
    <SelectControl>None</SelectControl>
  </Field>
</FieldGroup>
```

### Dropdown icon
Use dropdown-icon-type to control the presentation of the dropdown indicator. In a phone row the indicator is always the up-down chevrons of an iOS pop-up button, so this example is desktop only.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<SelectControl class="w-[200px]" selected :dropdown-icon-type="dropdownIconType" :StartIcon="CalendarAlt">Select date...</SelectControl>

<!-- Phone -->
<FieldGroup>
  <Field row label="Date">
    <SelectControl selected>Today</SelectControl>
  </Field>
</FieldGroup>
```

### Clearable
Listen to @clear-click to enable the clearable action. The clear button shows while the control is selected.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<SelectControl class="w-[200px]" selected @clear-click="value = ''" :StartIcon="CalendarAlt">{{ value || "Select..." }}</SelectControl>

<!-- Phone -->
<FieldGroup>
  <Field row label="Date">
    <SelectControl :selected="!!value" @clear-click="value = ''">{{ value || "None" }}</SelectControl>
  </Field>
</FieldGroup>
```

### Loading
During loading, the dropdown icon and clear action are automatically hidden, the control is marked aria-busy, and it ignores pointer and keyboard interaction. Use disabled to add visual disablement.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<SelectControl class="w-[200px]" selected :loading="loading" :StartIcon="CalendarAlt">Loading...</SelectControl>

<!-- Phone -->
<FieldGroup>
  <Field row label="Calendar">
    <SelectControl selected :loading="loading">{{ loading ? "Loading..." : "Work" }}</SelectControl>
  </Field>
</FieldGroup>
```

### Invalid
Use invalid to visually indicate the control is in an invalid state. A row has no frame to color: on Phone, pair invalid with the row's error-message.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<SelectControl class="w-[200px]" :invalid="invalid" selected>Pineapple on pizza</SelectControl>

<!-- Phone -->
<FieldGroup>
  <Field row label="Topping" :error-message="invalid ? 'Choose another topping.' : undefined">
    <SelectControl :invalid="invalid" selected>Pineapple</SelectControl>
  </Field>
</FieldGroup>
```

### Disabled
Use disabled to both visually and accessibly disable the control.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<SelectControl class="w-[200px]" :disabled="disabled" selected :StartIcon="User">Reader</SelectControl>

<!-- Phone -->
<FieldGroup>
  <Field row label="Role">
    <SelectControl :disabled="disabled" selected>Reader</SelectControl>
  </Field>
</FieldGroup>
```

### Block
Create full-width controls with the block prop. In a phone row the value sits at the row's end, so the row's control takes no block, and this example is desktop only.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<div class="w-[290px] text-center p-2 border border-dashed border-alpha/20 rounded-md">
  <SelectControl size="lg" :block="block" :StartIcon="CalendarAlt">Select...</SelectControl>
</div>

<!-- Phone -->
<FieldGroup>
  <Field row label="Date">
    <SelectControl>None</SelectControl>
  </Field>
</FieldGroup>
```

### Country selector
A plain country selector built with Select. The #triggerView and #optionView slots show flag icons with country names, no dial codes. Useful for profile forms, shipping addresses, and other country-only fields. countries holds {"{ value, label }"} options, and FlagIcon renders the flag image for a country code. The search box appears on its own because the list has more than 15 options; there is no prop for it.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<div class="w-[320px]">
  <Select
    v-model:value="country"
    :options="countries"
    placeholder="Select country..."
    :size="size"
    :variant="variant"
    :pill="pill"
    block
    search-placeholder="Search countries..."
    :list-min-width="300"
    :option-class-name="countryOptionClass"
  >
    <template #triggerView="{ value, label }">
      <span style="display: flex; align-items: center; gap: 8px; height: 100%; overflow: hidden; white-space: nowrap"><component :is="FlagIcon" :code="value" trigger />{{ label }}</span>
    </template>
    <template #optionView="{ value, label }">
      <span :class="countryLabelStyles.Root"><component :is="FlagIcon" :code="value" /><span :class="countryLabelStyles.Text">{{ label }}</span></span>
    </template>
  </Select>
</div>

<!-- Phone -->
<FieldGroup>
  <Field row label="Country">
    <Select
      v-model:value="country"
      :options="countries"
      :block="false"
      align="end"
      search-placeholder="Search countries..."
      :list-min-width="300"
      :option-class-name="countryOptionClass"
    >
      <template #optionView="{ value, label }">
        <span :class="countryLabelStyles.Root"><component :is="FlagIcon" :code="value" /><span :class="countryLabelStyles.Text">{{ label }}</span></span>
      </template>
    </Select>
  </Field>
</FieldGroup>
```

### Country code selector
A searchable country selector built with Select. The #triggerView and #optionView slots render flag icons and dial codes. A search-predicate matches both country name and dial code.
```vue
<!-- Desktop -->
<div class="w-[320px]">
  <Select
    v-model:value="country"
    :options="countryOptions"
    placeholder="Select country..."
    :size="size"
    :variant="variant"
    :pill="pill"
    block
    search-placeholder="Search countries..."
    :search-predicate="countrySearchPredicate"
    :list-min-width="300"
    :option-class-name="countryOptionClass"
  >
    <template #triggerView="{ value, label }">
      <span v-if="countryOf(value)" style="display: flex; align-items: center; gap: 8px; height: 100%; overflow: hidden; white-space: nowrap"><component :is="FlagIcon" :code="value" trigger />{{ countryOf(value).label }} ({{ countryOf(value).dialCode }})</span>
      <template v-else>{{ label }}</template>
    </template>
    <template #optionView="{ value, label }">
      <span :class="countryLabelStyles.Root"><component :is="FlagIcon" :code="value" /><span :class="countryLabelStyles.Text">{{ label }}</span></span>
    </template>
  </Select>
</div>

<!-- Phone -->
<FieldGroup>
  <Field row label="Country">
    <Select
      v-model:value="country"
      :options="countryOptions"
      :block="false"
      align="end"
      search-placeholder="Search countries..."
      :search-predicate="countrySearchPredicate"
      :list-min-width="300"
      :option-class-name="countryOptionClass"
    >
      <template #optionView="{ value, label }">
        <span :class="countryLabelStyles.Root"><component :is="FlagIcon" :code="value" /><span :class="countryLabelStyles.Text">{{ label }}</span></span>
      </template>
    </Select>
  </Field>
</FieldGroup>
```

### Phone form
Country selector with flag icons and phone input composed into a form. Selecting a country updates the dial code prefix, clears the phone field and focuses it. Phone numbers are formatted as you type using per-country patterns (e.g. 123 456 7890 for US, 1234 567 890 for UK). Search works by both country name and dial code.
