```vue
    name="email"
    :allow-autofill-extensions="allowAutofillExtensions"
    :placeholder="allowAutofillExtensions ? 'Allowed' : 'Not allowed'"
  />
</div>

<!-- Phone -->
<FieldGroup>
  <Field row orientation="vertical" label="Address">
    <Textarea
      :key="String(allowAutofillExtensions)"
      variant="ghost"
      name="address"
      autocomplete="street-address"
      :allow-autofill-extensions="allowAutofillExtensions"
      :placeholder="allowAutofillExtensions ? 'Allowed' : 'Not allowed'"
      auto-resize
      :rows="2"
    />
  </Field>
</FieldGroup>
```

## Toast
Toast notifications for transient feedback, with semantic variants, descriptions, action buttons, promise integration, and persistent toasts.
Import: import { Toaster, toast } from "@plexui/vue/components/Toast"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/toast
  Toaster:
    - position ("top-left" | "top-center" | "top-right" | "bottom-left" | "bottom-center" | "bottom-right") default: "top-center" — Where toasts anchor on the viewport
    - duration (number) default: 3000 — Default auto-dismiss duration in ms
    - offset (string | number | { top?; right?; bottom?; left? }) — Distance from the viewport edge
    - mobile-offset (string | number | { top?; right?; bottom?; left? }) default: 16px + safe area per edge — Mobile viewport gutter. Explicit edges override their safe defaults; omitted edges keep them.
    - gap (number) — Gap between stacked toasts in px

### Overview
```vue
<Button color="secondary" variant="outline" @click="toast('Inquiry ID copied')">
  Show toast
</Button>
```

### Variants
Semantic variants map to PlexUI's success / danger / warning / info tokens. No richColors is passed, so variants retheme automatically with data-theme.
```vue
<Button color="secondary" variant="outline" @click="toast('Changes saved')">Default</Button>
<Button color="secondary" variant="outline" @click="toast.success('Inquiry ID copied')">Success</Button>
<Button color="secondary" variant="outline" @click="toast.error('Failed to assign owner')">Error</Button>
<Button color="secondary" variant="outline" @click="toast.warning('Session expires in 2 minutes')">Warning</Button>
<Button color="secondary" variant="outline" @click="toast.info('New version available')">Info</Button>
```

### Description
```vue
<Button
  color="secondary"
  variant="outline"
  @click="toast('Changes saved', {
    description: 'Your profile is live for everyone on the team.',
  })"
>
  Show toast
</Button>
```

### Action
```vue
<Button
  color="secondary"
  variant="outline"
  @click="toast('Message sent', {
    description: 'Delivered to #design channel.',
    action: { label: 'Undo', onClick: () => toast.success('Message recalled') },
  })"
>
  Show toast with action
</Button>
```

### Promise
Use toast.promise to tie a single toast to an async operation. The toast shows a loading state, then swaps to success or error. Here saveProfile() resolves with {"{ name: \"Profile\" }"} after 1.5 seconds.
```vue
<Button
  color="secondary"
  variant="outline"
  @click="toast.promise(saveProfile(), {
    loading: 'Saving…',
    success: (data) => `${data.name} saved`,
    error: 'Save failed',
  })"
>
  Run promise
</Button>
```

### Long content
```vue
<Button
  color="secondary"
  variant="outline"
  @click="toast('Your deployment is still running', {
    description: 'This is a long message to show how the toast handles multi-line content without clipping or awkward wrapping. The title and description should both wrap cleanly.',
  })"
>
  Long content
</Button>
```

### Compact
Spread compact ({`import { toast, compact } from "@plexui/vue/components/Toast"`}) into any toast(...) options for a centered-text layout. Toast width shrinks to content by default regardless; the compact helper only adds center alignment for short status messages.
```vue
<Button color="secondary" variant="outline" @click="toast.success('Inquiry ID copied', compact)">
  Compact toast
</Button>
```

### Persistent
Pass duration: Infinity for toasts that stay open until the user acts on them. There are no close (×) affordances: put a labelled action on the toast so dismissal is intentional and keyboard-accessible.
```vue
<Button
  color="secondary"
  variant="outline"
  @click="() => {
    const id = toast('Backup scheduled for 2:00 AM UTC', {
      description: 'This stays open until you dismiss it.',
      duration: Infinity,
      action: { label: 'Dismiss', onClick: () => toast.dismiss(id) },
    })
  }"
>
  Persistent toast
</Button>
```

## Toolbar
"The top and bottom bars of a phone screen: navigation, a title, a search field and actions over the content. Desktop layouts have their own composition, so a toolbar never renders from 768px up."
Import: import { Toolbar, ToolbarLeading, ToolbarTitle, ToolbarTrailing } from "@plexui/vue/components/Toolbar"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/toolbar
Props:
  - placement ("top" | "bottom") default: "top" — Top bar with 44px targets, or bottom bar with 48px targets
  - titleStyle ("inline" | "large" | "compact-large" | "compact-medium") default: "inline" — Size and place of ToolbarTitle in a top bar
  - titleAlign ("center" | "start") default: "center" — Alignment of an inline title. Large and compact titles always start at the leading edge
  - background ("soft" | "hard" | "glass" | "solid") default: "soft" — How the bar meets the content scrolled under it, see Background. edge is the earlier name of soft
  - class (string) — Class of the bar
  ToolbarTitle:
    - subtitle (string) — Second, smaller line below the title. The subtitle slot takes precedence
    - as ("h1" | "h2" | "h3") default: "h1" — Heading level of the title

### Overview
Leading and trailing items take 44px targets; the title is centered across the whole width. Scroll the content: the canvas color fades out over the rows under the bar, and the glass starts to cast a shadow.
```vue
<Page surface="grouped">
  <Toolbar>
    <ToolbarLeading>
      <NavigationButton action="back" />
    </ToolbarLeading>
    <ToolbarTitle>Orders</ToolbarTitle>
    <ToolbarTrailing>
      <NavigationButton action="close" />
    </ToolbarTrailing>
  </Toolbar>
  <PageContent>...</PageContent>
</Page>
```

### Titles
title-style sets the size and place of the title: inline in the row of controls, large on a row of its own below them (on a page it scrolls up with the content under the controls, which then show it inline), and compact-large or compact-medium at the leading edge of the row. subtitle adds a second line. title-align="start" puts an inline title after the leading items.
```vue
<Toolbar title-style="large">
  <ToolbarLeading>
    <NavigationButton action="back" />
  </ToolbarLeading>
  <ToolbarTitle subtitle="24 orders">Orders</ToolbarTitle>
  <ToolbarTrailing>
    <NavigationButton action="confirm" />
  </ToolbarTrailing>
</Toolbar>
```

### Group
ToolbarGroup puts related actions in one capsule. A single action keeps a capsule of its own. Name each icon button with aria-label.
```vue
<Toolbar>
  <ToolbarLeading>
    <NavigationButton action="close" />
  </ToolbarLeading>
  <ToolbarTrailing>
    <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="Details"><InfoCircle /></Button>
    <ToolbarGroup aria-label="Order actions">
      <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="Translate"><Translate /></Button>
      <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="More"><DotsHorizontal /></Button>
    </ToolbarGroup>
  </ToolbarTrailing>
</Toolbar>
```

### Search
ToolbarContent fill stretches a search field between the leading and trailing items. The field takes the row height and the material of the bar buttons.
```vue
<Toolbar>
  <ToolbarContent fill>
    <Input variant="soft" pill placeholder="Search" aria-label="Search orders">
      <template #startAdornment>
        <Search />
      </template>
    </Input>
  </ToolbarContent>
  <ToolbarTrailing>
    <NavigationButton action="close" aria-label="Close search" />
  </ToolbarTrailing>
</Toolbar>
```

### Bottom
placement="bottom" sets the bar at the bottom of the page with 48px targets and the device's bottom safe area. ToolbarContent centers a main action; with fill a search field or a single action takes the whole row. The page content scrolls under both bars.
```vue
<Page surface="grouped">
  <Toolbar>
    <ToolbarTitle>Orders</ToolbarTitle>
  </Toolbar>
  <PageContent>...</PageContent>
  <Toolbar placement="bottom">
    <ToolbarLeading>
      <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="Filter"><Filter /></Button>
    </ToolbarLeading>
    <ToolbarContent>
      <Button color="primary" pill>New order</Button>
    </ToolbarContent>
    <ToolbarTrailing>
      <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="Search"><Search /></Button>
    </ToolbarTrailing>
  </Toolbar>
</Page>

<Toolbar placement="bottom">
  <ToolbarContent fill>
    <Button color="primary" pill block><Plus /> New order</Button>
  </ToolbarContent>
</Toolbar>
```

### Background
background sets how a bar meets the content scrolled under it, after the scroll edge effect of iOS 26. soft, the default, covers the content under the controls with the canvas color and fades it out past them over a light blur, with no line. hard is an opaque bar of the canvas color with a line. glass is a translucent, blurred bar with a hairline. solid is an opaque bar with no line. Hard and glass show themselves once content is under the bar: under the top bar after the content has scrolled, over the bottom bar while it continues below. PageHeader, PageFooter, the bars of a Sheet and TabBar take the same values.
```vue
<Page surface="grouped">
  <Toolbar background="hard">
    <ToolbarLeading>
      <NavigationButton action="back" />
    </ToolbarLeading>
    <ToolbarTitle>Orders</ToolbarTitle>
  </Toolbar>
  <PageContent>...</PageContent>
  <Toolbar placement="bottom" background="hard">
    <ToolbarContent>
      <Button color="primary" pill>New order</Button>
    </ToolbarContent>
  </Toolbar>
</Page>
```

### Disabled
A disabled bar button keeps its glass capsule and draws its symbol or label in the tertiary label color, as the iOS 26 bar buttons do. Inside ToolbarGroup the capsule belongs to the group, so the enabled actions next to it keep their look. A prominent action, NavigationButton action="confirm" or a solid primary Button, drops its color for a gray capsule, with a white label in light and a dim one in dark. Cancel and More stay enabled.
```vue
<Page surface="grouped">
  <Toolbar>
    <ToolbarLeading>
      <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" pill>Cancel</Button>
    </ToolbarLeading>
    <ToolbarTitle>New order</ToolbarTitle>
    <ToolbarTrailing>
      <NavigationButton action="confirm" disabled />
    </ToolbarTrailing>
  </Toolbar>
  <PageContent>...</PageContent>
  <Toolbar placement="bottom">
    <ToolbarLeading>
      <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="Filter" disabled><Filter /></Button>
    </ToolbarLeading>
    <ToolbarContent>
      <Button color="primary" pill disabled>Place order</Button>
    </ToolbarContent>
    <ToolbarTrailing>
      <ToolbarGroup aria-label="Order actions">
        <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="Translate" disabled><Translate /></Button>
        <Button color="secondary" variant="soft" uniform pill aria-label="More"><DotsHorizontal /></Button>
      </ToolbarGroup>
    </ToolbarTrailing>
  </Toolbar>
</Page>
```

### Sheet
In a Sheet the toolbar sits below the grabber and takes the place of the Sheet's own bar and Close button on a phone. Keep DialogHeader with DialogTitle: it still names the dialog for assistive technology, and it is the header from 768px up.
```vue
<Dialog v-model:open="open">
  <DialogTrigger>
    <Button color="secondary" variant="outline">Show orders</Button>
  </DialogTrigger>
  <DialogContent mobile-variant="sheet" surface="grouped">
    <Toolbar>
      <ToolbarLeading>
        <DialogClose>
          <NavigationButton action="close" />
        </DialogClose>
      </ToolbarLeading>
      <ToolbarTitle>Orders</ToolbarTitle>
    </Toolbar>
    <DialogHeader>
      <DialogTitle>Orders</DialogTitle>
    </DialogHeader>
    <DialogBody>
      ...
    </DialogBody>
  </DialogContent>
</Dialog>
```

## Tooltip
Brief informative text shown on hover or focus, for any trigger element, with conditional enabling and a compact variant.
Import: import { Tooltip, TooltipTriggerDecorator } from "@plexui/vue/components/Tooltip"
Docs: https://plexui.com/vue/components/tooltip
Props:
  - default slot (VNode) — The trigger: a single element. Wrap plain text in TooltipTriggerDecorator
  - content (string | number | VNode | null) — Tooltip content, or the #content slot (null = no tooltip)
  - compact (boolean) default: false — Short 1-3 word tooltips, inverse styling
  - interactive (boolean) default: false — Content is interactive, stays open when hovered
  - max-width (number | "none") default: 300 — Max width of tooltip
  - open-delay (number) default: 150 — Delay before showing (ms)
  - side ("top" | "right" | "bottom" | "left") default: "top" — Preferred side
  - side-offset (number) default: 5 — Distance from trigger
  - align ("start" | "center" | "end") default: "center" — Alignment
  - align-offset (number) default: 0 — Alignment offset
  - gutter-size ("sm" | "md" | "lg") default: "md" — Content padding
  - content-class-name (string) — Class for tooltip content
  - force-open (boolean) — Force open or closed
  - prevent-unintentional-click-to-close (boolean) default: false — Keeps the tooltip open when the trigger is clicked right after it opened
  - content slot (VNode[]) — Rich tooltip content; takes precedence over content

### Overview
```vue
<Tooltip content="This is additional context that appears when the trigger is hovered or focused">
  <TooltipTriggerDecorator>Simple text with tooltip</TooltipTriggerDecorator>
</Tooltip>
```

### Different triggers
Tooltip provides an optional component, TooltipTriggerDecorator, to help create accessible tooltip triggers. However, any component can be used as a trigger.
```vue
<div class="flex items-center gap-4">
  <Tooltip content="Tooltip on button">
    <Button color="primary" size="lg">Sample button</Button>
  </Tooltip>
  <Tooltip content="Tooltip on span">
    <span>Inaccessible trigger</span>
  </Tooltip>
</div>
```

### Conditionally enabled
Sometimes a tooltip should only be disabled under certain conditions, such as when a component is disabled, providing more context as to why that is. Below is an example of how to do this by conditionally passing in null content.
```vue
<Tooltip :content="disabled ? 'This action is disabled for reasons' : null">
  <Button color="primary" size="lg" :disabled="disabled">
    Sample button
  </Button>
</Tooltip>
```

### Compact tooltips
Compact tooltips are designed for short, 1-3 word tooltips with inverted styling. They feature minimal padding, a dark background with white text, and no box shadows. Use compact for brief status messages.
```vue
<Tooltip content="Copy to clipboard" compact>
  <Button color="secondary" variant="ghost" size="md" aria-label="Copy to clipboard"><Copy /></Button>
</Tooltip>
```
